He mentions the occurrence of the word [[ṣta|ṛta]] in I. 164. 47 and IV. 21. 3 ([[sadanat-Flasya|sadanāt-ṛtasya]]) and ‘[[stasya pathya|ṛtasya pathyā]]’ in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] III. [[12.7|12. 7]] and jumps to the conclusion that the last two words mean ’the path of truth’ and remarks that [[we|‘we]] have to find the path of Truth’ (Foreword p. XXX.). In the H. of Dh. vol. IV. pp. 2-5 it has been shown that [[șta|ṛta]] in the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] has three meanings, viz. (1) the regular and general order in the [[cosmos ;(2)|cosmos; (2)]] the correct and ordered way of the cult of the [[gods ;|gods;]] (3)‘moral conduct of [[man’|man.’]] In the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] [[sta|ṛta]] is not the same as ‘satya’, but the two are differentiated. In [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. 51. 2 the [[Visve-devas 1614|Viśve-devas]]172 are described as [[stadhitayaḥ|ṛtadhītayaḥ]] (whose thoughts are fixed on [[ṣta)|ṛta)]] and ‘satyadharmāṇaḥ’ (whose ordinances are true or fixed) and the sage prays to them to come to his sacrifice and to drink ([[ajya|ājya]] and soma) by the tongue of Agni. In [[Rg. X|Ṛg. X.]] 190. 1 both [[sta|ṛta]] and satya are said to have arisen from rigorous or refulgent tapas (of the Creator). In the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] [[sta|ṛta]] involved a very wide conception as stated above and ‘satya’ had a restricted [[moaning|meaning]] viz. truth or static order. In [[Rg. IX,|Ṛg. IX.]] 113. 4 Soma is described as one who proclaims [[sta,|ṛta,]] satya and [[średdhā|śraddhā]] (faith). Therefore, [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo is quite inaccurate when he [[translatos|translates]] [[ṣta|ṛta]] by the word [[Truth’|‘Truth’]] and draws important conclusions from his inaccurate renderings. Similarly, [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo does not give a correct rendering when he translates (Foreword p. XXX) the [[word |word ‘]][[stacit|ṛtacit]]’ as truth-conscious [[( whatever|(whatever]] that may mean [[sco.|acc.]] to [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo). Mr. Kapali Sastry (p. [[46 )|46)]] goes [[&|a]] step further than his great master by stating that in the mantras true [[know ledge|knowledge]] is termed [[stacit’|‘ṛtacit’]] [[Truth–consciousness|Truth-consciousness]] [[( with|(with]] a capital C). It appears that both Master and disciple are misled by the meaning of ‘cit’ in such an attribute as [[sat-cit-ananda’,|‘sat-cit-ānanda’,]] applied to brahma. They appear to take [[ta-cit’|‘ṛta-cit’]] as meaning two distinct things [[yta’|‘ṛta’]] and ‘cit’. The word [[‘rtacit’|‘ṛtacit’]] occurs [[P989]] five times in the [[Rg.;|Ṛg.;]] [[in 1615|in]]173 I. [[145.5,|145. 5,]] IV. [[3.4,|3. 4,]] [[V, 3.9(*ftacit|V. 3. 9 (‘ṛtacit]] is here an epithet of Agni), in [[VII 85.4|VII. 85. 4]] (it is an adjective of [[hots)|hotṛ)]] and in IV. 16. 10 it qualifies the word nārī [[( in|(in]] the context, [[Saci,|Śacī,]] the wife of Indra). The present author wonders whether [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo and his disciple cared to go into the meaning of [[*ptacit’|‘ṛtacit’]] in the several verses where it occurs. They pay no attention to the word [[‘ļṇacit’|‘ṛṇacit’]] that occurs in [[Rg. II,|Ṛg. II.]] 23. 17 as an attribute of [[Brahmanaspati. 1616.|Brahmaṇaspati.]]174

The space at the author’s disposal does not allow him to [[abow|show]] up the other unwarranted propositions and conclusions of [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo and Mr. Kapali Sastry. The author will set out the final conclusion of [[Sri.|Śrī.]] Aurobindo (Foreword p. XXIX) “what then is the secret meaning, the esoteric sense which emerges by this way of understanding the [[Veda ?|Veda?]] ……… The thought around which all is centred is the seeking after Truth, Light, Immortality. There is a truth deeper and higher than the truth of outward existence, a Light greater and higher than the light of human understanding which comes [[by:|by]] revelation and inspiration, an immortality towards which the soul has to rise. We have to find our way to that, to get into touch with this Truth and Immortality.” This is a grand peroration, but all this is built on shaky and meagre foundations, such as the wrong [[mean ings|meanings]] attached to the words [[ṣta|ṛta]] and [[ștaoit.|ṛtacit.]] Mr. Kapali Sastry [[( on|(on]] p. [[46 )|46)]] practically reproduces this grand summing up of his guru.

Mr. Kapali Sastry launches (on pp. [[22–26 )|22-26)]] a bitter diatribe against Sāyaṇa but ultimately he has to admit (pp. [[27–28 )|27-28)]] that Sāyaṇa is not merely useful, but indispensable to the students of the Veda. On p. 23 he translates a sūtra from Jaimini’s work [[the|‘the]] purpose of the Veda being ritualistic, words which do not have that significance are useless’ and remarks that this clearly lays down that the only purpose of the Vedas is that of ritual, those that do not pertain to that (ritualistic action) are worthless. The author doubts whether Mr. Kapali Sastry has carefully read the [[Purvamimānsāsūtras|Pūrvamīmāṁsāsūtras]] or has at least [[P990]] [[Correctly|correctly]] stated the position; what he quotes is the Pūrvapakṣa view [[( prima|(prima]] [[facie|facie]] view). Jaimini’s position is contained in the 7th [[sūtra 1617|sūtra]]175 [[as|‘as]] those passages (that do not directly concern themselves with ritualistic [[actions )|actions)]] form one syntactically connected whole with the passages prescribing [[ridhis|vidhis]] they are to be considered as commendatory of the vidhis’. Mr. Sastry is not satisfied with saying (on p. 8) [[the|‘the]] sages Madhucchandas and others are seers of the mantra, the Gods were present to the vision of these seers of old’, but he adds ’this seer of the beyond is [[alao|also]] the hearer of the truth; therefore that the poet-[[geors|seers]] are truth-hearers [[kavayah satyaśı utah|kavayaḥ satyaśrutaḥ]], is famous in the Veda’ (p. 64). The present writer wonders whether Mr. Sastry carefully read the original passages where the words [[kayayaḥ satyaśrutaḥ’occur|‘kavayaḥ satyaśrutaḥ’ occur]] in the Veda. The words [[kaziḥ|kaviḥ]] and [[karayaḥ|kavayaḥ]] occur several hundred times in the [[Rgveda,|Ṛgveda,]] but ‘satyaśrutaḥ’ occurs only thrice in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. 57. 8, V. [[58.8|58. 8]] and VI. [[49.6;|49. 6;]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] V. [[57, 8|57. 8]] and [[V.58.|V. 58.]] 8 are identical. In [[Rg. V.57. 8 and 1617a|Ṛg. V. 57. 8 and]]176 V. 58. 8 it is the wind-gods (Maruts) that are addressed as [[kavayah|kavayaḥ]] (wise) and [[salyaśrutah|satyaśrutaḥ]] (well-known as conferring true rewards) and not sages. VI. [[49.6|49. 6]] (first half of which is addressed to Parjanya and Vāta-wind god) runs ‘O [[esta blishers|establishers]] of the world! (O [[Maruts !)|Maruts!)]] that are wise and well-known as conferring true rewards, make the world turn towards the man by whose lauds you are praised’ (this half seems to be addressed to the band of Maruts). Further comment is [[superfluous. 1618|superfluous.]]177

[[P991]] The [[Mimānsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] made [[&|a]] sweeping generalisation that the whole Veda is meant for sacrifice. They went rather too far but they had substantial grounds for doing so. On pp. [[980–982|980-982]] of the H. of Dh. vol. [[II,|II.]] it has been shown how even the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] discloses that a complicated system of sacrifices with three savanas, numerous priests, three fires, existed in those far-off days and sacrifices like Atirātra ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VII. [[103.7)|103. 7)]] and Trikadruka ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. [[32.3,|32. 3,]] II. 11. 17, [[VIII, 13, 18,|VIII. 13. 18,]] [[VIII,|VIII.]] 92. 21, [[X 14, 16)|X. 14. 16)]] were being performed. The [[Mīmānsakas bad|Mīmāṁsakas had]] a long tradition behind them. But the case of [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo’s theories is entirely different. On flimsy grounds and mistaken meanings he builds up an imposing structure of an exoteric and also an esoteric meaning of vedic mantras, propounds that the sages wanted secrecy for their doctrines and all that they were concerned with was Truth, Light and [[Conscious1888.|Consciousness.]] It has been already shown that there are several philosophical and speculative hymns in the [[Rgveda,|Ṛgveda.]] But there is no motive of secrecy therein. If it is only Truth, Light and Consciousness with which the [[vedio|vedic]] sages were concerned ten thousand verses would not have been necessary. One should like to know what secret or higher or deeper esoteric Truth or Light or Consciousness to be kept concealed from common men is contained in hymns such as [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VII. 55 [[( sleeping|(sleeping]] charm), VII. 103 [[(maṇdāka-stuti),|(maṇḍūka-stuti),]] X. 34 (gambler’s song), X. 119 (exhilaration of power of Soma [[od|on]] Indra), [[X|X.]] 166 (invoking destruction of enemies), X. 190 (brief creation hymn), X. 191 (for concord and co-operation). Many more such hymns may be cited, where the theory of secrecy and Truth, Light and Consciousness would entirely fail. Besides, what is the secret (or spiritual or esoteric) element in such passages as those quoted above viz. I. 164. 46, X. [[129.%,|129. 2,]] VIII. [[58.2,|58. 2,]] which clearly express the most profound Truth. If the [[Mimāýsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] made a too wide generalization, [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo makes a far wider generalization with [[yory|very]] little basis.

The mantras of the [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] have a meaning and are not [[unmoaning|unmeaning]] letters as often in [[Tantrik|Tāntrik]] works. There is a [[discus sion|discussion]] in Nirukta [[I|I.]] [[15–16|15-16]] where it is said that in the absence of the Nirukta the apprehension of the meaning of the words used in mantras would not follow and the view of Kautsa is cited that the Nirukta is useless for understanding the meaning of mantras, since the mantras themselves have no sense (or are [[uselogs|useless]] or [[purposeless ).|purposeless).]] [[Yāska 1618|Yāska]]178 replies that mantras do possess [[P992]] a meaning since they employ the very same words that are used in ordinary Sanskrit, and then quotes a passage of the Ait. Br. [[(1.|(I.]] 5). [[Sabara|Śabara]] on Jai. I. 2. 41 states that where one cannot get at the meaning one has to come to some meaning on a [[con. sideration|consideration]] of other Vedic passages and on the basis of roots following Nirukta and Grammar.

One of the important [[topios|topics]] dwelt upon at length in the Purāṇas is that of avatāras. This emphasis on the conception of avatāras has greatly influenced the forms of religious worship, vratas and festivals. The subject of avatāras has been dealt with in the H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] vol. [[II,|II.]] pp. 717-724. It has been shown there that the beginnings of the doctrine of avatāras and of some of the well-known avatāras may be traced to the Vedic Literature viz. to the [[Satapatha-Brāhmana|Śatapatha-Brāhmaṇa]] (story of Manu and the fish in I. 8. 1. 1-6), story of [[Kurma|Kūrma]] [[(tortoise )|(tortoise)]] in [[Satapatha|Śatapatha]] VII. 5. 1. 5, of Varāha (Boar) in [[Sat.|Śat.]] Br. XIV. [[1.2,|1. 2,]] 11, [[Vamana|Vāmana]] (Dwarf) in [[Sat.|Śat.]] Br. I. 2. 5. 1 ff., [[Krspa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] son of [[Devaki,|Devakī,]] in Chān. Up. [[II,|III.]] 17. 6 and that the number of avatāras and the [[Dames|names]] also varied. But the treatment was not exhaustive and hence some details are added here from the [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] and from general considerations.

Avatāra (from root tṛ. with ‘ava’) means [[descending|‘descending’]] or [[descent’|‘descent’]] and the word is applied to gods, assuming the form of a human being or even of an animal and continuing to live in that form till the purpose for which that form was assumed was carried out. Reincarnation is one of the fundamental [[dootrines|doctrines]] of Christianity. But there is a vast difference between that doctrine and the Hindu theory. Re-incarnation in [[Chris tianity|Christianity]] is single and unique, while in the Hindu theory [[28|as]] adumbrated in the [[Gita|Gītā]] [[(IV.5-8)|(IV. 5-8)]] and the Purāṇas there have been many incarnations of the Deity and there may he many more in future. It is a comforting belief for [[ordinary mon|ordinary men]] to hold that when the affairs of the world are in a mess God comes down to the earth to set matters right. And this belief is held not only by the Hindus and Buddhists but by many peoples (including some in the rich and educated West) far apart from each other. Most Hindus, however, do not believe that great [[P993]] men, saints or prophets like [[Saṅkarācārya,|Śaṅkarācārya,]] Nānak, Shivaji or [[Mahatma|Mahātmā]] Gandhi are born again as avatāras in times of crisis. The Buddhists made Buddha in their Mahāyāna teachings go through many avatāras as bodhisattva before he attained [[Buddha hood.|Buddhahood.]] In modern times many persons pose or are made to pose as avatāras by their admirers or followers. Recently, Mr. J. G. Bennett (Hodder and Stoughton, 1958) has published a book called [[“Subud’|‘Subud’]] (Sushila, Buddha, Dharma) in which he suggests his firm belief that one Pak Subuh who hails from Indonesia is an avatar, the messenger from above for whom mankind is waiting. The Indian theory of avatāras is connected with the theory about yugas and [[manyantaras.|manvantaras.]] When the world is in [[gerious troubles|serious trouble,]] people believe that deliverance will come by the grace of God and they are often justified in their belief by the appearance of [[godly.|godly]] men who appear with some noble mission and masterly idea suited to the particular time and place when they [[appear,|appear.]]

In medieval and modern times the avatāras of Viṣṇu have been regarded as ten, viz. Matsya, Kūrma, Varāha, [[Nșsimha|Nṛsiṁha]] or [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] (Man-lion), Vāmana, Paraśurāma, [[Rama|Rāma]] (son of [[Dasaratha),|Daśaratha),]] [[Krṣṇa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] Buddha and Kalkin. The Varāha-purāṇa mentions these [[ton|ten]] in that [[order.1619)|order.]]179 In an inscription on the lintel above the figure of [[Śhaṅkara-Nārāyana|Śaṅkara-Nārāyaṇa]] in the [[Varāba Perumal 1620|Varāha-Perumal]]180 temple, [[thia Paurāṇik|this Paurāṇik]] verse except the first six letters (which cannot be read) is seen inscribed. The locus classicus about the descent of God in different [[formas|forms]] is in the [[Bhagavad gita 1621|Bhagavadgītā]]181 viz. ‘Whenever piety or righteousness (dharma) [[P994]] declines and [[impiaty|impiety]] grows up, I [[oreate|create]] myself. Age after age I am born for the protection of the good, for the [[destruc tion|destruction]] of evil-doers and the establishment of piety’. The [[game|same]] idea occurs in some of the parvans of the Mahābhārata, as in Vanaparva [[272.71|272. 71]] and [[Aśramedhika-parva|Āśvamedhika-parva]] 54. 13. None of the ten avatāras, except those of [[Krṣṇa|Kṛṣṇa]] and probably [[Rama,|Rāma,]] [[(“Rāmaḥ sastrabhṛtām-ahaun’,|(‘Rāmaḥ śastrabhṛtām-aham’,]] [[Gitā|Gītā]] X. [[31 )|31)]] are, however, [[men. tioned|mentioned]] by [[Dame|name]] in the [[Bhagavad-gitā.|Bhagavad-gītā.]] The number and names of the avatāras are not uniform in the Mahābhārata. In the [[Nārdyapiya|Nārāyaṇīya]] section of the [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] (chap. 339. verses 77-102) only six avatāras and their exploits are expressly mentioned, [[viz,|viz.]] Varāha (bringing up the earth submerged in the ocean), [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] (killing demon [[Hiraṇyakasipu ),|Hiraṇyakaśipu),]] Vāmana [[(van quishing|(vanquishing]] Bali and making him dwell in [[Patāla),|Pātāla),]] [[Bhargava Rama ( extirpating ksatriyas),|Bhārgava Rāma (extirpating kṣatriyas),]] Rāma, son of [[Dasaratha|Daśaratha]] (killing [[Ravaṇa),|Rāvaṇa),]] [[Krṣṇa ( killing|Kṛṣṇa (killing]] or vanquishing [[Kamsa, Narakasura, Bapa, Kalayavana,|Kaṁsa, Narakāsura, Bāṇa, Kālayavana,]] Jarāsandha, [[Siśupāla).|Śiśupāla).]] Then the same [[chapter 1622|chapter]]182 mentions [[ton|ten]] avatāras as follows: [[Hamsa,|Haṁsa,]] Kūrma, Matsya, Varāha, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] [[Vamana,|Vāmana,]] Rāma [[(Bhargava),|(Bhārgava),]] Rāma [[Dasarathi,|Dāśarathi,]] Sātvata, Kalki. Here Buddha is omitted and [[Kṭspa|Kṛṣṇa]] is called Sātvata and [[Hamsa|Haṁsa]] is added. In the [[Harivarsa 1623|Harivaṁśa]]183 it is said that the past avatāras have been thousands and in future also they would be thousands. [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] says the same thing. The [[Harivamsa|Harivaṁśa]] (I. 41. 27 ff) [[Dames|names]] only the following and their exploits, viz. [[Varāba,|Varāha,]] [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana, [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] Jāmadagnya [[(Parasurama),|(Paraśurama),]] Rāma, [[Kroṇa|Kṛṣṇa]] and [[Vedavyasa,|Vedavyāsa.]] But as [[Kośava|Keśava]] is said to be the 9th (I. [[41.6)|41. 6)]] it is to be [[under stood|understood]] that Matsya and Kūrma were counted, though not expressly named and Kalki [[Viṣpuyabas|Viṣṇuyaśas]] is mentioned as a [[P995]] [[futuro avatara.|future avatāra.]] The usual [[ton|ten]] avatāras are named in [[Vardha 1634|Varāha]]184 [[(4. %,|(4. 2,]] 48. 17-22, 55. 36-37), [[Mataya’|Matsya]] 285. 6-7, Agni [[(ohap.|(chap.]] 2-16 stories about all [[ton),|ten),]] [[Narasimhapurdna|Narasiṁhapurāṇa]] (chap. 36), Padma VI. 43. 13-15). In [[Vayu|Vāyu]] 98. 68-104 the text presents the avatāras in a confused manner and appears to mention [[ton|ten]] avatāras, viz. Varāha, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana, [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] [[Māndhātę,|Māndhātṛ,]] Jāmadagnya, [[Rama ( Dasarathi),|Rāma (Dāśarathi),]] Veda-vyāsa, [[Vasu deva,|Vāsudeva,]] Kalkin [[Vispuyaśas.|Viṣṇuyaśas.]] In [[Brahminda|Brahmāṇḍa]] III. [[73.75 #|73. 75 ff]] [[thote|there]] are lists of avatāras different from the present ten. In the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] the avatāras of Viṣṇu are mentioned in several places. In I. 3. 1-25 twenty-two avatāras including [[Brahma,|Brahmā,]] [[Dovarṣi|Devarṣi]] Nārada (who promulgated the [[Satvata|Sātvata]] system), [[Nara Nārāyana,|Nara-Nārāyaṇa,]] Kapila [[( who|(who]] taught to Asuri the [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] [[system ),|system),]] [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] [[Rsabha,|Ṛṣabha,]] (son of [[Nabhi|Nābhi]] and [[Merudeyi),1625|Merudevī),]]185 [[Dhanvan tari,|Dhanvantari,]] [[Mohini,|Mohinī,]] [[Vedavyāga,|Vedavyāsa,]] Balarāma and [[Kṛspa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] Buddha, Kalki are mentioned. In II. 7 twenty-three avatāras are mentioned, many of which are the same as in I. 3, but in II. 7 Dhruva, [[Pythu|Pṛthu]] son of Vena, [[Hayagriva|Hayagrīva]] are mentioned, the first two of which are mentioned as avatāras hardly anywhere else. In Bhāgavata [[XI,|XI.]] 40. 17-22 the following avatāras are mentioned, viz. Matsya, [[Hayasirsa,|Hayaśīrṣa,]] Kūrma, Sūkara, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana, [[Bhargava|Bhārgava]] Rāma, Rāma, Vāsudeva, Saṅkarṣaṇa, Pradyumna, Aniruddha, Buddha, Kalki. In [[Bhagavata XL|Bhāgavata XI.]] 4. 17-22 sixteen avatāras are set out, viz. usual ten plus [[Hamsa,|Haṁsa,]] Datta (Dattātreya), Kumāra (Nārada), [[Reabha,|Ṛṣabha,]] Vyāsa and [[Hayagriva.|Hayagrīva.]] Twelve avatāras are noted in Matsya 99. 14 and Padma V. 13. 182-186. The [[Prapancasāratantra|Prapañcasāratantra]] (ascribed to the great advaita teacher [[Saṅkarācārya)|Śaṅkarācārya)]] mentions [[( in|(in]] [[Patala|Paṭala]] 20. [[59 )|59)]] Matsya, Kūrma, Varāha, [[Nșsimha,|Nṛsiṁha,]] Kubja (i. e. [[Vamana),|Vāmana),]] three [[Ramas|Rāmas]] [[P996]] [[(i.e.|(i. e.]] [[Bhārgavarama,|Bhārgavarāma,]] Dāśarathi [[Rama|Rāma]] and [[Balarama),|Balarāma),]] [[Krona|Kṛṣṇa]] and Kalkin (i. e. it omits Buddha). The Ahirbudhnya [[Samhita|Saṁhita]] (5. 50-57) enumerates 39 avatāras of [[Vasudeva,|Vāsudeva,]] that are set out by Otto Schrader in his Introduction to the [[Panoarātra|Pañcarātra]] and the Ahirbudhnya [[Samhita,|Saṁhitā,]] pp. 42-43. The [[Viṣṇupurāṇa 1626|Viṣṇupurāṇa]]186 states that [[Lakṣmi|Lakṣmī]] follows Viṣṇu in his avatāras. The Purāṇas are full of the descriptions of the exploits of Viṣṇu in his several avatāras. But it should not be supposed that Śiva had no [[ayatāras.|avatāras.]] The Vāyu (chap. 23) mentions 28 [[avatārag|avatāras]] of Maheśvara the last of which was [[Nakuli (Lakuli)|Nakulī (Lakulī)]] as verse 221 [[gays.|says.]] In Varāha 15. 10-19 all avatāras are praised except that of Buddha. But [[Varāba ( 48,|Varāha (48.]] 20-22) provides that worship of [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] frees men from the fear of sins, of [[Vamana|Vāmana]] leads to removal of delusion, of [[Parasurama|Paraśurāma]] to wealth, one should worship Dāśarathi [[Rama|Rāma]] for the destruction of cruel enemies, one desirous of a son should worship [[Balarama|Balarāma]] and [[Kșsna,|Kṛṣṇa,]] one who desires a handsome form should worship Buddha and one should worship Kalkin for the slaughter of [[enemies 1627|enemies.]]187 The Agnipurāṇa (chap. 49. 1-9) describes what characteristics the images of the ten avatāras should possess and says that the image of Buddha should be represented as having a quiet face, long ear-lobes, fair complexion, wearing an upper garment, seated in Padmāsana posture and his hands should have the varada and abhaya poses.

From the facts that Kṣemendra in his [[Dasavatāra-carita 1628|Daśāvatāra-carita]]188 (composed in 1066 [[A, D.),|A. D.),]] and the [[Gitagovinda|Gītagovinda]] of Jayadeva (court poet of [[Laksmanasena)|Lakṣmaṇasena)]] mention the [[ugual ton|usual ten]] avatāras with Fish as the first and that the Matsya passage about ten avatāras is quoted by Aparārka (first half of 12th century), it follows that all the ten avatāras of Viṣṇu had become recognised throughout India at least about the 10th century A. D.

[[P997]] Kumārila (7th century A. D.) did not accept Buddha as an avatāra, though about that [[contury|century]] some people had come to recognize him as such (vide note 1629). Besides, as shown above, there were many views about the total number of avatāras, their names and the order in which they appeared. Vide Dr. Katre’s paper in Allahabad University Studies, vol. X. pp. [[37–130|37-130]] for discussion on 33 avatāras. The [[Varāba|Varāha]] avatāra is mentioned in the Eran stone [[Boar 1629|Boar]]189 Inscription of Toramāṇa (Gupta inscriptions pp. 158-160) in the first quarter of the 6th century. The [[Raghuvansa|Raghuvaṁśa]] (IV. 53 and [[58 )|58)]] refers to the recovery of land near the Sahya mountain from the Western Sea by Rāma [[(Bhargava);|(Bhārgava);]] vide pp. 89-90 and note 224 above for references in the [[Mahabharata|Mahābhārata]] and [[Purāpas|Purāṇas]] to Paraśurāma’s exploits. The [[Sarvānukramani|Sarvānukramaṇī]] p. 42 on [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X, 110 names as [[ļṣi|ṛṣi]] Jamadagni or his son Rāma The Meghadūta mentions the planting of the left foot of Viṣṇu on Bali (i.e. the [[Vamana|Vāmana]] avatāra). Māgha in [[Sisupala-vadha|Śiśupāla-vadha]] (XV. 58) regards Bodhisattva (Buddha) as an avatāra of Hari and as sought to be tempted by the hordes of Māra. [[Māgha 1630|Māgha]]190 flourished about 725-775 A. D. The [[know ledge|knowledge]] of [[Vamana|Vāmana]] and [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] avatāras can be carried back centuries before the Mahābhāṣya of Patañjali, since it refers to works and dramatic representations of the imprisonment of Bali and the killing of [[Kamsa|Kaṁsa]] (vide pp. 130, 203 and notes 330, 521 above). In the Daśāvatāra cave at Ellora one sees the [[repre sentations|representations]] of Varāha, [[Narasimha,|Narasiṁha,]] Vāmana and [[Krsna.|Kṛṣṇa.]] Those caves are ascribed to the 8th century [[A. D.1631|A. D.]]191 Therefore, it appears almost certain that some at least [[( viz.|(viz.]] [[Vamana,|Vāmana,]] [[Parasu rāma|Paraśurāma]] and [[Krsna)|Kṛṣṇa)]] of the usual [[ton|ten]] avatāras had been recognised some centuries before Christ and all ten had come to be recognised by some writers and other people before the 7th century A. D.

[[P998]] The conception of avatāras contributed largely to the increase of Dharmaśāstra material. They gave rise to numerous vratas and festivals. For example, the [[Varāhapurāpa|Varāhapurāṇa]] devotes [[chaptera|chapters]] 39-48 to [[dvādaśi-vratas|dvādaśī-vratas]] in honour of the [[ton|ten]] avatāras from Matsya to Kalkin. There are separate festivals called [[Jayantis|Jayantīs]] of the avatāras such as [[Narasimha-jayanti|Narasiṁha-jayantī]] on [[Vaisakha|Vaiśākha]] śu. 14, [[Paraśurāma jayanti ( on Vaiśākha su. 3).|Paraśurāma-jayantī (on Vaiśākha śu. 3).]] Vide pp. [[262 263|262-263]] for the tithis and months in which the different avatāras [[appeared. 16316|appeared.]]192

Descriptions of each of the ten well-known [[avatūras|avatāras]] occur in several Purāṇas; for example, Matsya avatāra is described in Matsya chap. 1-2, Agni 2, [[Narasimua|Narasiṁha]] 37; Kūrma in Agni 3, Bhāgavata I. 3. 16, VIII. 7-8-10, [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] 38; [[Varāba|Varāha]] in Matsya 247-248, Vāyu 6. [[11–26,|11-26,]] Bhāgavata III. 13. 18-45, III. 19. 25-30, [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] 39; [[Narasimha avatara|Narasiṁha avatāra]] in Brahma 58. 12 ff., [[213.43|213. 43]] ff., Matsya 161-163, Bhāgavata I. 3. 18, VII. 8. 18, [[Narasimha-purāṇa|Narasiṁha-purāṇa]] 41-44; [[Vimana|Vāmana]] in Brahma 73 and 213. 80-185, [[Vāgu|Vāyu]] 98, 74-87, Vāmanapurāṇa [[78.51,|78. 51,]] [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] VIII. 18. 12 to [[VIII,|VIII.]] 22. 33, [[Narasimla|Narasiṁha]] 45; [[Parasurama|Paraśurāma]] in Matsya [[244–246,|244-246,]] [[Bhagavata IX,|Bhāgavata IX.]] 15. 13, [[IX,|IX.]] 16. 1-26 and vide above pp. 89-90 and note 204; Rāma in Brahma 176, Agni 5-11 (the seven [[kāndas|kāṇḍas]] of the Rāmāyaṇa are summarised), Vāyu 88. [[183 198,|183-198,]] [[Bhag.|Bhāg.]] IX. 10-11, Padma [[IV.1-68,|IV. 1-68,]] [[Narasiinha|Narasiṁha]] 47-52; [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] in Brahma 14-17, [[180-21%,|180-212,]] Agni 12-15, Bhāg. X. 3, 44-45, [[50 52|50-52]] &c., [[XL|XI.]] 1 and 30, [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] 53; Buddha in Brahma 180. 27-39, Agni [[16,|16.]] 1-3, [[Varāba|Varāha]] 180. [[27–29,|27-29,]] [[213,|213.]] 32 ff., Padma VI. 31. 13-15, [[Bhag. I 3.24;|Bhāg. I. 3. 24;]] [[Kalkin -|Kalkin —]] Vide H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] III. pp. [[923 925|923-925]] for references. The avatāras and their [[jayantis|jayantīs]] are described in many Dharmaśāstra works, but the [[ļodarānanda,|Ṭoḍarānanda,]] vol. I. edited by Dr. P. L. Vaidya in the Ganga Oriental Series probably contains the longest account of the [[ton|ten]] avatāras [[(pp. 39-386 ).|(pp. 39-386).]] Thousands of verses are devoted by the Purāṇas to each of the topics of dāna (gifts), [[sraddha,|śrāddha,]] [[tirtha|tīrtha]] and vrata and they have been quoted at [[longth|length]] by works on Dharmaśāstra. It would be [[P999]] best to bring together by way of illustration a few of the pages of the previous volumes of the History of Dharmaśāstra on these subjects.

Vide vol. II. pp. 880-881 (for gifts of certain dānas called dhenus from Matsya chap. 82, Varāha, chap. 99-110, Agni, chap. 210), p. 882 (for gifts called parvata or meru from Matsya 83-92, Agni 210 quoted by Aparārka pp. 344-454), pp. 884-885 (for grahaśānti from Matsya 93 and about images of planets from Matsya [[84 ),|84),]] p. 892 [[( for|(for]] dedication of a reservoir of water to the public from Matsya 58), pp. [[895–896|895-896]] for the planting of trees and the dedication of a garden to the public [[( Matsya|(Matsya]] 59, Agni 70), pp. 896-899 (for dedication of temples and consecration of images in Matsya [[264–266,|264-266,]] Agni 60 and 66); vol. IV. pp. [[162–164|162-164]] about various hells (Agni 203, 371, Brahma 22, 214-215, Brahmavaivarta, [[Prakrtikhanda|Prakṛtikhaṇḍa]] 29 and 33, Nāradapurāṇa I. 15, Padma [[IV,|IV.]] 227, Bhaviṣya, Brāhmaparva 192, [[Bhāgayata|Bhāgavata]] V. 26, Viṣṇu V. 6, [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] 12 and 14); vol. IV. p. 170 about heaven being really happiness of the mind (Brahma 22. 44 and 47, Viṣṇu IV. 6. 46); [[VOL|vol.]] IV. pp. [[177–178|177-178]] on the doctrine of [[Karmevipāka ( Vāmana|Karmavipāka (Vāmana]] 12, [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] 15, Varāha 203, [[Viṣṇu dharmottara|Viṣṇudharmottara]] II. 102); vol. IV. p. 181 on the signs of approaching death [[( Vayu|(Vāyu]] 19, [[Linga|Liṅga]] 91, Mārk. 43. 29-39 or chap. 40 of Venk. ed. and [[Viṣṇudharmottara II, 218);|Viṣṇudharmottara III. 218);]] vol. IV. p. 212 on cremation (Varāha 187, Garuḍa II. 4); vol. IV. p. 256 on the persons entitled to perform funeral rites ([[Viṣṇu II,|Viṣṇu III.]] 13, Mārk. chap. 30 of B. I. ed. and 27 of [[Vonk,|Venk.]] ed.); vol. IV. pp. [[261–262|261-262]] the rites after death being called pūrva or nava, madhyama (navamiśra) and uttara (or purāṇa) in Viṣṇupurāṇa [[II. 13;|III. 13;]] vol. IV. p. 265 about an [[ativāhika|ātivāhika]] body being [[29gumed|assumed]] by the soul of a dead person (Brahma 214, [[Mārk,|Mārk.]] 16, Agni 230 and 371); vol. IV. p. 272 on [[aśauoa ( impurity|āśauca (impurity]] on birth and death) from Kūrma II. 23, [[Linga|Liṅga]] I. 89, Garuḍa, [[protakhanda|pretakhaṇḍa]] 5, Agni [[157–158,|157-158,]] Vāmana 14. It is unnecessary to refer to the pages of this volume itself for numerous references to Purāṇas as regards tithis, various vratas, times for religious rites, astrological matters [[&o.|&c.]]

It should not be supposed that the Purāṇas are restricted to [[topios|topics]] called religious in popular parlance and to the five [[charac teristio|characteristic]] topics of creation, re-creation [[( |(]]sarga, pratisarga &c.) Some of the Purāṇas contain an exhaustive treatment of the duties of kings, ministers, commander-in-chief, judge, envoy scribes, court [[physioian|physician]] and of coronation, invasion [[&o|&c]]

[[P1000]] Many of these matters have already been dealt with in vol. III. of the H. of Dh. The most exhaustive treatment of what may be called political matters is found in Matsya chap. 215-226 and 240, Agni [[218–242,|218-242,]] Viṣṇudharmottara [[II.|II,]] chap. 2-7, 18-21, 24-26, 28, 61-63, [[66–72,|66-72,]] [[145–152,|145-152,]] 177. Among other Purāṇas Garuḍa I. 108-115, [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] 24 (in Venk. [[od,|ed,]] or 27 in [[Bagerji’s|Banerji’s]] ed.), Kālikā 87 contain some discussion of matters political. It should be noticed that both Matsya [[( 240.2)|(240. 2)]] and Agni [[(228.1)|(228. 1)]] employ the two technical words, [[‘Akranda’|‘Ākranda’]] and [[‘Pārspigrāha’|‘Pārṣṇigrāha’]] that are part of the theory of [[Mandala|Maṇḍala]] in [[Kautilya|Kauṭilya]] (VI. 2. p. 260) explained in H. of Dh. vol. III. p. 222. The Matsyapurāṇa is profusely quoted by one of the earliest extant digests on Dharmaśāstra viz. the [[Kṛty& kalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] on Rājadharma (pp. 23, 25-30, 34-38, [[42–43,|42-43,]] 55-61, 118-122, [[123–124,|123-124,]] 158-161 and many verses from chap. 227 and 241 of the Matsya on the administration of justice in [[Vyavahāra kānda|Vyavahārakāṇḍa]] pp. 342, 345, 348, 377, 406, 409-10, 562, 581, 594-95, 599). The same digest quotes many verses [[(pp. 9–13,|(pp. 9-13,]] 128, 166, 178-181) from the Brahmapurāṇa which are not found in the printed Brahma ([[Ādan,|Ānan.]] ed.), but which are quoted by the [[Rājanīti. prakāśa|Rājanītiprakāśa]] of Mitramiśra (borrowing from [[Kr̥tyakalpataru)|Kṛtyakalpataru)]] pp. 138, 158, 283, 416-419 and some by the Rājadharmakaustubha of [[Anantadova|Anantadeva]] [[(pp. 326–330).|(pp. 326-330).]] The Viṣṇudharmottara on rājadharma is not quoted in the Kṛtyakalpataru on [[rajadharma,|rājadharma,]] but it is frequently quoted by the Rājanītiprakāśa e. g. Vi. Dh. II. [[18.1,|18. 1,]] 5-14 are quoted by R. N. P. on pp. 32-33, Vi. Dh. II. 18. 2-4 are quoted by R. N. P. p. 61, [[Vi. Dh. II,|Vi. Dh. II.]] 22. 1-185 are quoted by R. N. P. pp. 66-81 (on mantras to be recited and the several divinities to be invoked at a king’s coronation), [[Vi, Dh.|Vi. Dh.]] II. 23. 1-13 are quoted by R. N. P. on pp. 82-83 (describing the [[towards|rewards]] of the mantras recited in Vi. Dh. II. 22). Vi. Dh. is quoted 21 times by the [[Rajadharma-kaustubha.|Rājadharma-kaustubha.]] These three Purāṇas alone ([[Mataya,|Matsya,]] Agni, [[Viṣoudharmottara )|Viṣṇudharmottara)]] devote several thousand verses to the topic of rāja-dharma and allied matters. The Garuḍa-purāṇa (I. 108-115) devotes about four hundred [[yerges|verses]] to [[Rājansti|Rājanīti]] (political thought) but many of them are like subhāṣitas (bon mots) and are borrowed from Manu (e. g. Garuḍa I. 109. 1 and 52, [[110.7,|110. 7,]] [[115 63|115. 63]] are respectively Manu [[VII,|VII.]] 213, VIII. 26, [[II,|II.]] 239, IX. 3), the Mahābhārata, the [[Nāradasmrti|Nāradasmṛti]] (e. g. ’na sā sabhā’ in Garuḍa [[115.52|115. 52]] is Nārada III. [[18),|18).]] The Garuḍa itself states that it will expound the essence of [[niti|nīti]] ([[Rajadharma)|Rājadharma)]] based upon [[P1001]] [[Artham sāstra 1632|Arthaśāstra]]193 and the like; while the colophons at the end of chapters 108-114 [[sver|aver]] that they contain the [[Nitisāra|Nītisāra]] promulgated by Bṛhaspati. One verse is almost the same as the [[Introduotory|Introductory]] verse 5 of Bāṇa’s [[Kadambari 1633|Kādambarī]]194 and I am disposed to hold that it is the Garuḍa that is the borrower.

Certain verses from the [[Mārkandeya-purāṇa|Mārkaṇḍeya-purāṇa]] (24. 5, 23-33 or chap. 27 and 21-31 in Banerji’s ed.) are quoted by R. N. P. pp. 30-31 [[( about|(about]] the duties of kings and their acting in the peculiar ways of five gods, viz. Indra, Sūrya, Yama, Soma and Vāyu). The Dāyabhāga quotes the [[Markandeya-purāṇa|Markaṇḍeya-purāṇa]] for pointing out that [[sāpindya|sāpiṇḍya]] in the matter of inheritance and succession is different from [[sāpiṇdya|sāpiṇḍya]] for the periods of aśauca [[( impurity|(impurity]] on [[death 1634|death]]195). The Kṛtyakalpataru on Rājadharma [[(pp. 182-183 )|(pp. 182-183)]] quotes a passage from Skandapurāṇa on the festival of [[Kaumudimahotsava|Kaumudīmahotsava]] to be celebrated by the king. The same passage is quoted by R. N. P. pp. 419-421.

The [[Kr̥tyakalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] (on [[Rāja dharma)|Rājadharma)]] quotes a long passage (pp. 201-212) from Bhaviṣyapurāṇa [[on. |on ‘]]Vasordhārā’, which is quoted by the R.N.P. [[(pp. 447-457 )|(pp. 447-457)]] from the [[Devipurāṇa.|Devīpurāṇa.]] The [[Kālikāpurāpa|Kālikāpurāṇa]] in chapter 87 [[dovotos|devotes]] 131 verses to [[Rājaniti,|Rājanīti,]] in which a summary is furnished of the course of conduct that should be followed by the king. This chapter expressly mentions the works of Uśanas and [[Bphaspati|Bṛhaspati]] (verses 99 and 130) and advises the king to honour brāhmaṇas that are advanced in knowledge, learning, tapas and age, to control his senses, to employ the four upāyas (sāma, dāna, [[danda|daṇḍa]] and [[bheda ),|bheda),]] to avoid the vices of gambling, drinking, indulgence in sexual matters and hunting, to practise the six guṇas [[( yāna,|(yāna,]] āsana [[&c. ),|&c.),]] to test the princes, councillors, the queens and other female relatives by upadhās [[( investigation|(investigation]] of character by various [[trioks) 1635|tricks).]]196

[[P1002]] [[. It|It]] appears that [[Kautilya’s|Kauṭilya’s]] Arthaśāstra was not available to most medieval writers and therefore they relied on the purāṇas for the treatment of [[Rajadharma.|Rājadharma.]] But the early [[Puranas|purāṇas]] like the Matsya appear to have made use of [[Kautilya’s|Kauṭilya’s]] work. Vide the author’s paper on [[‘Kautilya|‘Kauṭilya]] and the Matsya-purāṇa’ in Dr. B. C. Law presentation volume II. pp. 13-15.

In the matter of the administration of justice and the law of [[SUCCession|succession]] and inheritance also some of the Purāṇas have influenced the views of writers of nibandhas. The [[Kr̥tya kalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] on Vyavahāra quotes about twelve verses from the Kālikā-purāṇa on the marks of a truthful party or witness and on the appropriate ordeals in the case of the several [[varṇas, 1636|varṇas,]]197 their procedure and the different causes of action (vide pp. 79, 205, 210, 211, 221, 231, 238). Three verses from the Kālikā (chap. 91. 35-37) about the twelve kinds of sons and the [[impro priety|impropriety]] of making a punarbhava, [[svayandatta|svayaṁdatta]] and a dāsa successor to a kingdom are quoted in the R. N. P. pp. 35 and 42. [[Verges|Verses]] 38-41 of Kālikā, chapter 91, about the sons that can be adopted and the age up to which they can be adopted are quoted by the [[Dattakamimamsā|Dattakamīmāṁsā]] p. 60 [[( Ānan.|(Ānan.]] ed.) and by the [[Vyava haramayūkba|Vyavahāramayūkha]] p. 114, though the latter remarks that they were not found in two or three mss. of the [[Purāṇa, 1637|Purāṇa.]]198 In connection with the question of the seniority among [[twing|twins]] the [[Mayakha|Mayūkha]] quotes [[Sridhara’s|Śrīdhara’s]] comment on Bhāgavata (III. 19. 18) that the son born first is the younger one; but the [[Vyavahāramayūkha1638|Vyavahāramayūkha]]199 makes the interesting remark that in the Purāṇas practices opposed to the [[smrtis|smṛtis]] are frequently [[soon.|seen.]]

[[d ai agrariaci ***TESTATOTT g aat ag i Huan I. 4. 25 g. by ṣi p. 37, which remarks agar artar षगमाद्धारतस्य घेदकार्यात्मज्ञानकारित्वसिद्धिः’. नीशूदबिजबन्धूनां न वेदश्रवणं मतम् । तेषामेव Feature Front pana al Teatre|स्त्रीशूद्रद्विजबन्धूनां त्रयी न श्रुतिगोचरा । … तस्माद्भारतमाख्यानं कृपया मुनिना कृतम् ॥ भागवत I. 4. 25 q. by परिभाषाप्रकाश p. 37, which remarks ‘वेदकार्यकारित्वावगमाद्भारतस्य वेदकार्यात्मज्ञानकारित्वसिद्धिः’. स्त्रीशूद्रद्विजबन्धूनां न वेदश्रवणं मतम् । तेषामेव हितार्थाय पुराणानि कृतानि च । देवीभागवत]] I. 3. 21. [[T er|शङ्कराचार्य]] is careful to point out on Vedāntasūtra I. 3. 38 that śūdras have no [[ao|no]] adhikāra for [[brahmavidya|brahmavidyā]] based on the study of the Veda. But he does not deny to the śūdras the knowledge of the Self altogether. He refers to the instances of Vidura and Dharmavyādha that were possessed of the knowledge of brahma due to the effects of their former lives, states that they would secure the result of [[brabma jñāna|brahma-jñāna]] (viz. mokṣa, [[fipal|final]] liberation from saṁsāra), that śūdras have the right to learn from the [[Mahabbārata|Mahābhārata]] and Purāṇas as stated in “he should read to the four varṇas’ and that in that way they might secure knowledge of brahma and mokṣa. [[” pat ga: attitainetega rad स्तेषां न शक्यते फलमातिः भतिषेद्धं ज्ञानस्यैकान्तिकफलसात् । ‘श्रावयेच्चतुरो वर्णान! इति चेतिहासपुराणाधिगमे चातुर्वर्ण्यस्याधिकारस्मरणात् । वेदपूर्वकस्तु नास्त्यधिकारः बाणामिति Rua Oy on a. C. I, 3, 38. On . . III, 4. 36 yuan refers to the woman वाचक्रषी as one who had the knowledge of brahma ‘रैक-वाचक्नवी qua T ATO ret: 1, faren|” येषां पुनः पूर्वकृतसंस्कारवशाद्विदुरधर्मव्याधप्रभृतीनां ज्ञानोत्पत्तिस्तेषां न शक्यते फलप्राप्तिः प्रतिषेद्धुं ज्ञानस्यैकान्तिकफलत्वात् । ‘श्रावयेच्चतुरो वर्णान्’ इति चेतिहासपुराणाधिगमे चातुर्वर्ण्यस्याधिकारस्मरणात् । वेदपूर्वकस्तु नास्त्यधिकारः शूद्राणामिति स्थितम् ॥ भाष्य on वे. सू. I. 3. 38. On वे. सू. III, 4. 36 शङ्कराचार्य refers to the woman वाचक्नवी as one who had the knowledge of brahma ‘रैक्व-वाचक्नवीप्रभृतीनामेवंभूतानामपि ब्रह्मवित्त्वश्रुत्युपलब्धेः ‘. गार्गी वाचक्नवी]] figures as a great seeker after brahma in the Bṛ. Up. III. 6. 1, III. 8. 1 and 12. The [[HP|महाभारत]] says that what it [[dilaten|dilates]] upon as to the पुरुषार्थs, धर्म, अर्थ, काम and मोक्ष, is found elsewhere and that what it does not contain on those subjects can be found nowhere else and that the [[Mabābhārata|Mahābhārata]] should be listened to by [[bim|him]] who desires mokṣa, by [[brāhmagas,|brāhmaṇas,]] kings and pregnant women [[(Svargārohang parva|(Svargārohaṇaparva]] 5. 50-51). ↩︎

[[एणु मोहिनि महाक्य घेदीयं|शृणु मोहिनि मद्वाक्यं वेदोयं]] बहुधा स्थितः । यज्ञकर्मक्रिया [[घेवः स्मृतिको महाभमे ॥ स्पतिवेदः क्रियायेदः|वेदः स्मृतिर्वेदो गृहाश्रमे ॥ स्मृतिर्वेदः क्रियावेदः]] पुराणेषु प्रतिष्ठितः । [[पुराणपुरुषाजातं यथेदं जगवतम् । वये पाखाय जात पुराणेभ्यो|पुराणपुरुषाज्जातं यथेदं जगदद्भुतम् ॥ तथेदं वाङ्मयं जातं पुराणेभ्यो]] न संशयः । वेदार्थादधिकं मन्ये पुराणार्थं वरानने [[। वेदाः प्रविधिमा सर्ने|॥ वेदाः प्रतिष्ठिताः सर्वे]] पुराणेष्वेव सर्वदा । [[विभेत्यल्पश्चताबेदो मामयं महरिष्यति ॥ न वेदे महसधारोन दिः काल बोधिनी ।|बिभेत्यल्पश्रुताद्वेदो मामयं प्रहरिष्यति ॥ न वेदे ग्रहसञ्चारो न शुद्धिः कालबोधिनी ।]] तिथिवृद्धिः क्षयो वापि पर्वग्रहविनिर्णयः ॥ [[इतिहासपुराणस्त निश्चयो त पूरा दिवेदेषु तत्सर्व लक्ष्यते सती । उभयोर्यन र हितपुराणैः मणीयते। “पदैनीम पत्र उपागीयते । पुराणैः स्मृतिमिव वेद एव नियते । पुराणमन्यथा मत्था निर्मायोनिमयाप्त पात् ।|इतिहासपुराणैस्तु निश्चयोयं कृतः पुरा । यन्न दृष्टं हि वेदेषु तत्सर्वं लक्ष्यते स्मृतौ ॥ उभयोर्यन्न दृष्टं हि तत्पुराणैः प्रणीयते । … यद्वेदैर्गीयते सुभ्रु उपाङ्गैर्यत्प्रगीयते ॥ पुराणैः स्मृतिभिश्चैव वेद एव निगद्यते । पुराणमन्यथा मत्वा तिर्यग्योनिमवाप्नुयात् ॥]] नारदीयपुराण II. 24. 15-24. I. 5.1.1), ‘सामना पासवश्विदत् (है.सं.1, 1.1), ‘देवा देवयजनमभ्यवसाय विशो नमामानन्।.सं.|रुद्रत्वम्’ (तै. सं. I. 5. 1. 1), ‘स आत्मनो वपामुदक्खिदत्’ (तै. सं. V. 3. 12. 2),]] [[’#grereppe fragt frantaa ari. #. V, 2. 7.|‘न पृथिव्यामग्निश्चेतव्यो नान्तरिक्षे न दिवि । तै. सं. The reply is: [[‘ranta YAE ardal frentat el: ’ ( 6. 1.2.7)|‘विधिना त्वेकवाक्यत्वात् स्तुत्यर्थेन विधीनां स्युः’ (जै. I. 2. 7)]] [[1. e,|i. e.]] they are [[landatory|laudatory]] and are meant to praise vidhis. ↩︎

Some of the verses are striking and they are therefore quoted here: [[ज्यायन्कृते यजन्य नेतायो|ध्यायन्कृते यजन्यज्ञैस्त्रेतायां]] द्वापरेऽर्चयन् । [[यदामोति तदामोति कलौ संकीय केशवम्|यदाप्नोति तदाप्नोति कलौ संकीर्त्य केशवम् ॥]] [[धर्मोत्कर्षमतीवात्र पामोति|धर्मोत्कर्षमतीवात्र प्राप्नोति]] पुरुषः कलौ । [[अल्पायासेन धर्मज्ञास्तेन तुष्टोस्म्यहं काले, “जयन्ति से निजाँलोकान् केशेन महता द्विजाः। द्विजाभूषवेष पाकयज्ञाधिकारवान । निजाचायति लोकान सूबो धन्यतरस्ततः॥ भक्ष्याभल्येषु नास्यास्ति पेयापेयेषुबै पतः । नियमो निशाला सेनासौ सावितीरितः। “योषिकुषणावर्तुः कर्मणा मनसा गिरा । तद्धिता गुममामोति वसा लोक्यं यतो द्विजाः । नातिक्लेशेन महता सानेव पुरुषो यथा । तृतीयं व्याहतं तेन मया साचिति योषितः “स्वल्पेन हि प्रयत्नेन धर्मः सिष्यति वै कलो। नरैरात्मणाम्भोभिः शालिवाखिल किल्मिषेः॥ शुबैश्च विजयुषा तत्परैविजसत्तमाः । वथा नीभिरनायासात् पतिजभूषयैव हि। ततत्रितयमप्येतन्मम धन्यतरं मतम् । धर्मसम्पादने क्लेशो द्विजातीनां कृताविषु। विष्णुपु.|अल्पायासेन धर्मज्ञास्तेन तुष्टोस्म्यहं कलेः ॥ … जयन्ति ते निजाँल्लोकान् क्लेशेन महता द्विजाः ॥ द्विजशुश्रूषयैवैष पाकयज्ञाधिकारवान् । निजाञ्जयति वै लोकान् शूद्रो धन्यतरस्ततः ॥ भक्ष्याभक्ष्येषु नास्यास्ति पेयापेयेषु वै यतः । नियमो मुनिशार्दूलास्तेनासौ साध्वितीरितः । … योषिच्छुश्रूषणाद्भर्तुः कर्मणा मनसा गिरा । तद्धिता शुभमाप्नोति तत्सालोक्यं यतो द्विजाः ॥ नातिक्लेशेन महता तानेव पुरुषो यथा । तृतीयं व्याहृतं तेन मया साध्विति योषितः ॥ … स्वल्पेन हि प्रयत्नेन धर्मः सिध्यति वै कलौ । नरैरात्मगुणाम्भोभिः क्षालिताखिलकिल्बिषैः ॥ शूद्रैश्च द्विजशुश्रूषा तत्परैर्द्विजसत्तमाः । तथा स्त्रीभिरनायासात् पतिशुश्रूषयैव हि । ततस्त्रितयमप्येतन्मम धन्यतरं मतम् । धर्मसम्पादने क्लेशो द्विजातीनां कृतादिषु ॥ विष्णुपु०]] VI. 2. 17-18, 22-24, 28-28, 34-36. The [[कल्पतरू|कल्पतरु]] (गृहस्थकाण्ड pp. 272-273) quotes from [[पिण्Y VI. 3.22-24 पारतन्त्र्य"रितः (तम् .1.).|विष्णु VI. 3. 22–24 पारतन्त्र्य…रितः (तम् v. l.).]] The editor of कल्पतरु was unable to trace these. ↩︎

S.]] Turberville [[( Home|(Home]] University Library, 1932), who is constrained to say on p. 235 that at the best the Holy Office in Spain [[bas|has]] a terrible record of destruction; Cambridge Medieval History vol. VI, [[cbap.|chap.]] XX on [[‘Harestes|‘Heresies]] and the Inquisition in the Middle [[Agos’|Ages’]] (1929) pp. 699–726; Cambridge Medieval History vol. VI. pp. 694–695 where it is shown that [[’ Indulgencor’|‘Indulgences’]] [[(grantiog|(granting]] forgiveness of sins and a certificate of entry in Paradise) were freely put on sale by the highest ministers of the Christian Church in the [[bands|hands]] of licensed traders without the necessity of any confession and [[penit ence|penitence]] and that they became a formidable bar to the proper working of [[tha”|the]] penitential system of the Church. ↩︎

    [[· A|A]] few books for those [[wbo|who]] want to know more of Buddhism in general and of [[Hinayāna|Hīnayāna]] and [[Mabāyāna|Mahāyāna]] are recommended here. [[The|‘The]] central conception of Buddhism’ (London, 1923), ’the conception of [[Nirvana”|Nirvāṇa’]] (Leningrad, 1927), ‘Buddhist Logic’ vol. I (1958), all by Th. [[Stcherbatsky:|Stcherbatsky;]] [[‘Vedantic Boddhism|‘Vedantic Buddhism]] of the Buddha’ by J. G. Jennings (Oxford [[Uo.|Un.]] Press, [[1948):|1948);]] [[Creed|‘Creed]] of Buddha’ by Edmond Homes [[( 5th ed.):|(5th ed.);]] [[Introduction|‘Introduction]] to Tantrik Buddhism’ by Dr. [[Shasbi|Shashi]] Bhushan Das-Gupta (Cal. [[Voiversity,|University,]] 1950), ’the [[Flane|Flame]] and the Light’ by [[Hagh I,|Hugh I.]] Faussett (London and New York, 1958); [[“the|‘the]] Buddha and his Dhamma’ by Dr. B. [[R,|R.]] Ambedkar (1957) in which he refrains from considering Buddhist Texts except those in Pali; ‘Comparative study of Buddhism and [[Christianity,|Christianity’]] by [[Prof, F. Masutan!|Prof. F. Masutani]] (Tokyo, 1957). The [[Mabāyāna-sūtrālankāra|Mahāyāna-sūtrālaṅkāra]] of [[Asanga|Asaṅga]] (ed. by Prof. S. Levi) [[sommarises la|summarises in]] two verses (I. 9-10) the points (five) on which the two schools are in conflict. It-sing’s [[Records|‘Records]] of the Buddhist [[religion|religion’]] translated by Dr. J. [[Takakasa|Takakusu]] (Oxford, 1896) surprisingly enough states (p. 15) ‘These two systems are perfectly [[accord|in accord]] with the noble [[doctrine,|doctrine.]] Both equally conform to truth and lead us to [[Nirvana’,|Nirvāṇa’.]] [[Baddha|Buddha]] at least ignored (if he did not positively deny) God, [[be|he]] denied the individual soul and Eternity, [[be|he]] did not emphasize the most vital [[Upa nigad|Upaniṣad]] teaching viz. [[’ anando brabmeti vyajāgāt’.|‘ānando brahmeti vyajānāt’.]] He imagined salvation as [[&|a]] state of [[absolate|absolute]] quiescence and therefore regarded ordinary life as misery and asserted that salvation may be attained even in this very life. He did not claim to be God, but a [[buman|human]] being. There are various kinds of Mahāyāna doctrines and great diversity of definitions. It may generally be said that works professing to teach [[Mahāyāaa|Mahāyāna]] practically forsake the ideal of a human Buddha, preach the [[worsbip|worship]] of Buddha and [[fatore|future]] Buddhas, and assert that [[Nirvana|Nirvāṇa]] cannot be attained by the ancient method, that salvation cannot come in this very life but after centuries and [[acons|aeons]] of the practice of [[virtas|virtue]] and helping others.

    Mantrayāna and [[Vajrayana|Vajrayāna]] are said by some to be branches of [[Maba gina,|Mahāyāna,]] about the latter of which a good deal will be said in the next section. According to Bhikṣu [[Rabula Sāṅktyāyada,|Rāhula Sāṅkṛtyāyana,]] [[Vajrayana ( 700-1200 A.D. )|Vajrayāna (700-1200 A.D.)]] is [[2|a]] synonym of [[Mabāyāna|Mahāyāna]] (400–700 A. D.) and [[werely|merely]] the ulterior [[dopalope ment|development]] of it (vide p. 211 of the paper ‘L’ Origine du [[Vajrayina ot Laa|Vajrayāna et Les]] 84 [[Siddhar’ io|Siddhas’ in]] J. A. vol. 225 (1934) pp. 209-230. ↩︎

[[17.4;|17. 4;]] [[आचार्यकुलादेवमधीत्य “स्वाध्यायमधीयानो धार्मिकान्विदधदात्मनि सन्द्रियाणि संप्रतिक्षाप्याहिंसन् सर्वभूतान्यन्यत्र तीर्थभ्यः “न|आचार्यकुलाद्वेदमधीत्य … स्वाध्यायमधीयानो धार्मिकान्विदधदात्मनि सर्वेन्द्रियाणि संप्रतिष्ठाप्याहिंसन् सर्वभूतान्यन्यत्र तीर्थेभ्यः … न]] च पुनरावर्तते । छा. उप. VIII. [[15:|15;]] [[तदेतत्वर्य शिबम हान वयामिति|तदेतत्त्रयं शिक्षेद्दमं दानं दयामिति]] । [[वृह.|बृह.]] उप. [[V.2.|V. 2.]] ↩︎

  78. दया सर्वभूतेषु [[शान्तिरनसूया|क्षान्तिरनसूया]] शौचमनायासो मङ्गलमकार्पण्यमस्पृहेति । यस्यैते [[चत्वारिंशसंस्कारा|चत्वारिंशत्संस्कारा]] न [[चाटावाल्मगुणा|चाष्टावात्मगुणा]] न [[सब्रह्मणः|स ब्रह्मणः]] सायुज्यं सलोकतां गच्छति । [[गो.ध..|गौ. ध. सू.]] VIII. 24-25. The मत्स्य [[(52.8-11)|(52. 8–11)]] sets out these very [[atmaganas|ātmaguṇas]] that Gautama mentions[[,|;]] vide also मार्कण्डेय 25. 32-33 for almost the same [[aight ganas|eight guṇas]] including [[abimasā.|ahiṁsā.]] ↩︎

  79. अहिंसा परमो धर्मः [[सर्वप्राणमतां पर|सर्वप्राणभृतां वर]] । [[तस्मात्माणमृतः सर्वात हिस्यात् बाणा|तस्मात्प्राणभृतः सर्वान्न हिंस्याद् ब्राह्मणः]] [[कचित्|क्वचित्]] ॥ आदिपर्व 11. [[13-14:|13-14;]] अहिंसा सर्वभूतेषु [[धर्म|धर्मं]] ज्यायस्तरं [[वितुः|विदुः]] । द्रोण [[192.38;|192. 38;]] अहिंसा [[सर्वसूतेग्यो धर्मेन्यो|सर्वभूतेभ्यो धर्मेभ्यो]] ज्यायसी [[मता।|मता ।]] शान्ति 265. 6; न हिंस्यात्सर्वभूतानि [[मैत्रायणगतवरेत्|मैत्रायणगतश्चरेत्]] । शान्ति 329. 18; अहिंसा परमो धर्मस्तथाहिंसा परं तपः । अहिंसा परमं [[सस्य|सत्यं]] यतो धर्मः [[प्रवर्तते।|प्रवर्तते ॥]] [[अदशासन|अनुशासन]] [[115.25;|115. 25;]] अहिंसा परमो [[धनों|धर्मो]] हिंसा [[चाधर्मलाणा। आवमेधिक|चाधर्मलक्षणा । आश्वमेधिक]] 43. 21. ↩︎

  80. अहिंसा [[सत्यमस्तेय वान क्षान्तिईमः|सत्यमस्तेयं दानं क्षान्तिर्दमः]] शमः । [[अकार्पण्य|अकार्पण्यं]] च [[शौच|शौचं]] च [[तपन रजनी घरवशाल|तपश्च रजनीचर । दशाङ्गो]] राक्षसश्रेष्ठ धर्मोऽसौ [[सार्ववणिकः|सार्ववर्णिकः]] [[।|॥]] वामन [[14. 1-27न|14. 1-2; न]] वेदैर्न च [[वाचन वपोनिर्म चाम्बरः। कथंचित् स्वर्गति|दानैश्च न तपोभिर्न चाध्वरैः । कथंचित् स्वर्गतिं]] यान्ति पुरुषाः [[माणिहिंसका:|प्राणिहिंसकाः ।]] अहिंसा परमो धर्मो [[हिसव|ह्यहिंसैव]] परं [[सा।|तपः ।]] अहिंसा [[परम|परमं]] [[दानमित्याहुमुनयः|दानमित्याहुर्मुनयः]] [[सदा।|सदा ।]] मशकान् [[सरीसपान वंशान्यूकायान्मानयास्तथा आलो पम्येन|सरीसृपान् दंशान्यूकाद्यान्मानवांस्तथा । आत्मौपम्येन]] पश्यन्ति मानवा ये [[दयालयः। पम I.31.|दयालवः ॥ पद्म I. 31.]] 26-28; these verses are repeated in [[पा|पद्म]] VI.   81. [[se va luftest I WAT: fra: e t राणाम् ।|अहिंसा सत्यमस्तेयं ब्रह्मचर्यापरिग्रहौ । यमाः संक्षेपतः प्रोक्ताश्चित्तशुद्धिप्रदा नृणाम् ।]] कर्मणा मनसा वाचा सर्वभूतेषु सर्वदा । अक्लेशजननं [[मोक्का बहिंसा परमर्षिभिः।|प्रोक्ता त्वहिंसा परमर्षिभिः ॥]] [[अहिंसाया: परो घमों नास्त्यहिंसापर|अहिंसायाः परो धर्मो नास्त्यहिंसापरं]] सुखम् । विधिना या [[भवेद्धिसा त्वहिसैव प्रकीर्तिता कर्म|भवेद्धिंसा त्वहिंसैव प्रकीर्तिता ॥ कूर्म]] II. 11. 13-15. The [[(chap.|लिङ्गपुराण (chap.]] 8. 8–9) enumerates the [[clgbt|eight]] [[sadhanas|sādhanas]] of [[oth|योग]] of which [[yo|यम]] is first and [[mentiods|mentions]] five as [[lo n.|in कूर्म.]] Yamas (abstinences) are variously [[coomerated.|enumerated.]] Kūrma appears to follow the [[II.|योगसूत्र II.]] 30-31 ‘अहिंसा-सत्य-अस्तेय-[[ब्रह्मचर्यापरिबहा|ब्रह्मचर्यापरिग्रहा]] यमाः । [[शोच-सन्तोष-सपा स्वाध्याय ईश्वरणि una fayat: 11.|शौच-सन्तोष-तपः-स्वाध्याय-ईश्वरप्रणिधानानि नियमाः ।’.]] [[Maga IV,|Manu IV.]] 204 provides generally that one should always practise yamas and that one may not always practise the [[alyamas,|niyamas,]] but does [[pot pame|not name]] [[them,|them.]] [[Medhatitbi|Medhātithi]] explains that yamas are prohibitions [[( viz, aot to [njare|(viz. not to injure]] life, not to steal, [[aot|not]] to tell an [[untrath,|untruth,]] not to have forbidden [[Berdal|sexual]] intercourse and not to possess wealth that belongs to another or not to accept gifts), [[whilo|while]] niyamas consist of positive [[acte,|acts,]] such as one should always study the Veda (as [[la Madu|in Manu]] IV. 147). [[Yaj. ni.|Yāj. III.]] 312–313 [[saamerates ton|enumerates ten]] yamas viz. celibacy, compassion, forbearance, charity, [[abianca|absence]] of crooked conduct, [[ahimsā,|ahiṁsā,]] [[not-steallag,|non-stealing,]] [[sweatness,|sweetness,]] restraint of [[Bon|senses]] and [[teo alyamas,|ten niyamas.]] The [[TR-FHITE|वैखानस-स्मार्तसूत्र]] (IX. 4) [[enamoratos tan|enumerates ten]] yamas. ↩︎

[[यस्मित|यस्मिंस्तु]] निहते [[भदे|भद्रे]] जीवन्ते [[पहषः मुखम्|बहवः सुखम्]] । [[तस्मिएहते|तस्मिन् हते]] नास्ति [[मे पावकं चोप पातकम्|शुभे पातकं चोपपातकम् ॥]] ब्रह्माण्ड II. 36. 188, [[पायु|वायु]] 69. 162 (reads लभन्ते for जीवन्ते).   101. सांख्यं योगः [[पाश्चरात्रं|पाञ्चरात्रं]] [[घेवाः|वेदाः]] पाशुपतं तथा । ज्ञानान्येतानि राजर्षे [[विधि Familia tn.|विद्धि नानामतानि वै ॥ शान्ति]] 349. 64; but later on it is said [[pic474 1 pre Trafat:|सांख्यस्य वक्ता कपिलः परमर्षिः]] स [[उच्यते।|उच्यते ।]] [[“उक्तवानिवमव्यग्रो|… उक्तवानिदमव्यग्रो]] ज्ञानं पाशुपतं शिवः । [[पाश्चरात्रस्य कुस्नस्य घेत्ता|पाञ्चरात्रस्य कृत्स्नस्य वेत्ता]] तु भगवान् [[pret mai guste Tray Eau la|स्वयम् । सर्वेषु च नृपश्रेष्ठ ज्ञानेष्वेतेषु दृश्यते ॥ शान्ति]] 349. 65-68 q. by [[far|परिभाषाप्रकाश]] p. 21 [[( which|(which]] reads [[a TE 4).|वक्ता तु भगवान् स्वयम्).]] These verses are borrowed almost verbatim by the योगियाज्ञवल्क्य XII. 5-6 and the same work reads ‘[[सांखयं|सांख्यं]] योगं [[***Ti 14Arraifa anat faaruri|… तथा । अतिप्रमाणान्येतानि हेतुभिर्न विचारयेत् ।’]] XII. 4 (q. by [[tecro|अपरार्क]] on [[p11),|p. 11),]] which means that the five systems are beyond the ordinary means of [[koow ledge|knowledge]] and they should not be disturbed by mere ratiocination. ↩︎

  102. सांख्यं [[योग|योगं]] पञ्चरात्रं शैवं पाशुपतं तथा । कृतान्तपञ्चकं विद्धि [[बह्मणः|ब्रह्मणः]] परिमार्गणे ॥ [[Perguntargo 1.|विष्णुधर्मोत्तरपु० I.]] 74, 34 q. by [[ft. on ara|हे. on व्रत]] vol. I. p. 25 and [[aft:1974#TE .|परिभाषाप्रकाश p.]] 22. ↩︎

  103. Why the [[colt|cult]] of [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] was called [[Pāācarātra bas aot beeg|Pāñcarātra has not been]] satisfactorily explained anywhere in the English Histories of Indian [[Philo sophy|Philosophy]] so far as I know. The name leads to the inference that the cult has something to do with five matters. But why is the word rātra or kāla used? That is the difficulty. [[Io Sāati|In Śānti]] 336. 46 (cr. ed. 323. 42) [[Pañca. satra|Pañcarātra]] is referred to as [[. Pañcatala’|‘Pañcakāla’]] ([[ante: quo terbat:),|‘तैरिष्टः पञ्चकालज्ञैर्हरिरेकान्तिभिर्नरैः’).]] [[Sevoral|Several]] guesses have [[bean|been]] put forward, some of which are: (1) on five nights [[Nārāyana|Nārāyaṇa]] taught Ananta, Garuḍa, [[Viśvakseoa,|Viśvaksena,]] Brahmā [[sad|and]] Rudra; (2) The [[Parama-sambita|Parama-saṁhitā]] [[(31. 19 )|(31. 19)]] ‘states that God imparted this doctrine in five nights to four sages, [[Sadatkumāra, Sadaka, Sabandana and Sanatana;|Sanatkumāra, Sanaka, Sanandana and Sanātana;]] (3) this cult blackened (rātri is dark) five teachings, viz. [[164, M, QEVE, her and m ai|सांख्य, योग, पाशुपत, बौद्ध and आर्हत;]] (4) this [[(pāñcarātra )|(pāñcarātra)]] teaches five aspects [[, , fara (1. 8. mare), auttaa, sraf|पर, व्यूह, विभव (i. e. अवतार), अन्तर्यामिन्, अर्चा]] (images); (5) it dwells upon five duties of [[Valgavas viz,|Vaiṣṇavas viz.]] tāpa (branding on the arm and other limbs), [[puṇdra|puṇḍra]] ([[up right|upright]] lines made on the forehead with some colouring substance), [[dāma|nāma]] (names of [[Vasudeva ), wantra|Vāsudeva), mantra]] (like ‘Om namo Nārāyaṇāya), yāga (worship of images of [[Vāsodeva ).|Vāsudeva).]] The [[Alwar|Ālwar]] literature mentions fivefold [[aature viz, Pars|nature viz. Para]] and the others. Vide K. [[Č.|C.]] Varadachari’s paper on [[Some contri butions|‘Some contributions]] of [[Alwars|Ālwars]] to the philosophy of bhakti’ in Silver [[Jabilee|Jubilee]] vol. of BORI, p. 621. The [[Paramasa óbitā|Paramasaṁhitā]] [[(1.|(I.]] 39-40 [[G.O. S.|G. O. S.]] ed.) states that the five great Elements, the five [[Tanmatras, Abamkāra, Baddhi and aupakta, ( five|Tanmātras, Ahaṁkāra, Buddhi and avyakta, (five]] categories or Tattvas of the [[Saṅkhya )|Sāṅkhya)]] are the night [[( as it were )|(as it were)]] of the [[Puran|Puruṣa]] and therefore this [[lastra|śāstra]] (which propounds how to be free from the [[moshes|meshes]] of these [[five )|five)]] is called [[Pancarātra.|Pāñcarātra.]] ↩︎

  104. The four sūtras in the Vedāntasūtra (II. 2. 42-45) are: [[para, a nd rura, ferate an aquaru:, fardura.|उत्पत्यसम्भवात्, न च कर्तुः करणम्, विज्ञानादिभावे वा तदप्रतिषेधः, विप्रतिषेधाच्च.]] Though [[Rāma puja|Rāmānuja]] enlarges upon the [[Pancarātra|Pāñcarātra]] doctrine in his comments on the last two sūtras out of these four and quotes three passages that may be called [[Panca rātra,|Pāñcarātra,]] he does not avow either in his [[Srībbāsya|Śrībhāṣya]] or in [[bis|his]] [[Vedārtha-saigraha|Vedārtha-saṅgraha]] that he is a Sātvata or Pāñcarātra. ↩︎

  105. वेदविप्रतिषेधश्च भवति । [[चतुर्ष|चतुर्षु]] [[घेवेषु|वेदेषु]] परं [[श्रेयोऽलग्या|श्रेयोऽलब्ध्वा]] शाण्डिल्य इदं [[शासमाधि Taurorullarreara 1 SITE T164|शास्त्रमधिगतवानित्यादिवेदनिन्दादर्शनात् । शाङ्करभाष्य]] on [[II, 2. 43.|वेदान्तसूत्र II. 2. 45.]] [[Fyrard beglas’ E HITTE Argan (on TEE|शङ्कराचार्य begins ‘तत्र भागवता मन्यन्ते’ (on ब्रह्मसूत्र]] II. 2. 42) and [[vader n.|under II.]] 2. 44 he [[rem marks|remarks]] ‘[[नच पचरात्रसिद्धान्तिभिर्वासदेवाविवकस्मिन् सर्वेषु पा ज्ञानेश्वर्याविवारतम्यता कश्चिदोन्युपगम्यते।।.|न च पञ्चरात्रसिद्धान्तिभिर्वासुदेवादिष्वेकस्मिन् सर्वेषु वा ज्ञानैश्वर्यादितारतम्यकृतः कश्चिद्भेदोऽभ्युपगम्यते ।’.]] It may further [[he|be]] noted that in the [[Santiparve पाशराब|Śāntiparva पाञ्चरात्र]] is called [[her|सात्वतधर्म]] (chap. 348. 34 and 84). [[Cros la his granta ( 8th great ),|बाण in his हर्षचरित (8th उच्छ्वास),]] while describing the persons professing various religious and [[pbilosophie cal|philosophical]] doctrines gathered near the great [[acārya|ācārya]] Divākara-mitra, separately [[men tions|mentions]] Bhāgavatas and [[Pañcaratrikas|Pāñcarātrikas]] ‘[[foutue Payout: forfatteren सुमन कापिलैजैनलोकायातक"पौराणिकैः सासवन्तः शैवैः शादः पाशराधिकरण्यैश्चात्र MET ME FATRATEL quafi|विटपच्छायासु निषण्णैः … भागवतैर्वर्णिभिः केशलुञ्चनैः कापिलैर्जैनैर्लोकायतिकैः … पौराणिकैः साप्ततन्तवैः शैवैः शाब्दैः पाञ्चरात्रिकैरन्यैश्चात्र स्वान् स्वान् सिद्धान्तान् शृण्वद्भिः]] &c.’. What Bāṇa probably means is that [[14|भागवत]] stands for the general cult of bhakti (in the [[Gitā )|Gītā)]] and [[for|पाञ्चरात्र for]] one school among [[pants,|भागवतs,]] that had as its characteristic the doctrine of four [[vyūbas.|vyūhas.]] This is like [[UTE OUTHTHUTRITT.|ब्राह्मणश्रमणन्याय.]] The [[Tiara|वृद्धहारीतस्मृति]] [[(11,|(11.]] 181–192.) states that [[Sagdilya|Śāṇḍilya]] composed a work for performing the worship of [[Vigga|Viṣṇu]] by a [[non Vedic|non-Vedic]] procedure, that [[Vimu|Viṣṇu]] cursed him to remain in hell but [[rolanted|relented]] when [[Sandilya|Śāṇḍilya]] threw himself on [[bis|his]] mercy and reduced the period of [[holl-residence|hell-residence.]] ↩︎

  109. It is remarkable that the [[Bhagavata,|Bhāgavata,]] [[wbich|which]] is practically the most important or sole authority for all great medieval [[Vaigava|Vaiṣṇava]] teachers like Vallabha and Caitanya and their disciples, is not quoted anywhere by [[Rāmā nuja|Rāmānuja]] ( who was born in [[sake|śake]] 1049 i. e. 1127 A. D.) in his [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]] on the Brahmasūtras, when he quotes over a hundred verses from the Viṣṇupurāṇa in the same. In fact, in the [[Vedārthsaigraba|Vedārthasaṅgraha]] Rāmānuja states that [[la|in]] the [[name|same]] way as the section (anuvāka) on Nārāyaṇa among all [[fruti|śruti]] texts serves to expound the special aspect of highest Brahma so the [[Vigopurāga|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] also defines a special aspect of the highest Brahma and that all other [[Purīgas|Purāṇas]] should be so interpreted as [[aot|not]] to be in conflict with it [[at hele tatt केवलपरमाखरूपविशेषगदर्शनापैव प्रात्तो नारापणादवाकस्तयेवं वैष्णवं च पुराण"परमहा स्वरूपविशेषनिर्णयायैव प्रवृत्तम् । अन्यानि सर्वाणि पुराणान्येतदविरोधेन नेयानि। पेवार्थसंग्रह|‘यथा सर्वासु श्रुतिषु केवलपरब्रह्मस्वरूपविशेषप्रदर्शनायैव प्रवृत्तो नारायणानुवाकस्तथैवं वैष्णवं च पुराणं … परब्रह्मस्वरूपविशेषनिर्णयायैव प्रवृत्तम् । अन्यानि सर्वाणि पुराणान्येतदविरोधेन नेयानि । वेदार्थसंग्रह]] para 110-111 pp. 141-142 (D. C. ed. 1956). [[THEO meations (in weet on a r II. 2. 41 and 45 ) HochfEAT, HIGHET|रामानुज mentions (in भाष्य on वेदान्तसूत्र II. 2. 41 and 45 ) पौष्करसंहिता, सात्वतसंहिता]] and [[Tropicar|परमसंहिता]] among the [[पाश्चरावसंहिताs,|पाञ्चरात्रसंहिताs,]] but does nowhere [[afirm|affirm]] that he is a [[tollower|follower]] of the [[परात्र|पञ्चरात्र]] [[doctring.|doctrine.]] There are numerous commentaries on the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] and [[com mentaries|commentaries]] on commentaries (Das Gupta in vol. 4 pp. 1-2 lists over 40 commentaries on the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]]). It is [[donecessary|unnecessary]] in this work to refer to the numerous commentaries of the disciples and followers of Madhva and other great [[Valsnava|Vaiṣṇava]] ācāryas. The position of Vallabhācārya ( 1479–1531 A. D.) appears to be that the [[Bhagavata|Bhāgavata]] is the supreme authority in case of doubt [[पेदा श्रीकृष्णवाक्यानि ग्यासस्वाणि चैवाहि।समाधिभाषा व्यासस्य प्रमाण तश्चाष्टयम्। Tu g iterary natate fpe|‘वेदाः श्रीकृष्णवाक्यानि व्याससूत्राणि चैव हि । समाधिभाषा व्यासस्य प्रमाणं तच्चतुष्टयम् । उत्तरं पूर्वसन्देहवारकं परिकीर्तितम् ॥ तत्त्वदीपनिबन्ध]] (Ahmedabad, 1926); vide also Prof. G. H. Bhatt in I. H. Q. vol. IX, 300-306. [[Vallabbācārya’s|Vallabhācārya’s]] is called [[g (moaning Foot FAR)|पुष्टिमार्ग (meaning कृष्णानुग्रह)]] and he holds that even bhakti [[ia|is]] difficult to accomplish in the Kali age. ↩︎

  139. [[Palkasas|Pulkasas]] and [[Svapākas|Śvapākas]] were degraded and untouchable [[castes,|castes.]] Vide H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] vol. II pp. 88-89 for Pulkasa and p. 97 for [[Svapāka|Śvapāka]] (lit. who subsists on [[dog-ment).|dog-meat).]] Pulkasa occurs in Vāj. S. 30. 17. Kirātas are assigned to caves in [[vaj.|Vāj.]] S. 30. 16. The [[Abbīras|Ābhīras]] are described as dasyus and mlecchas in Mausala-parva 7. 46-63, they are said to have attacked Arjuna in Pañcanada when he was taking women with him after [[Krona’s|Kṛṣṇa’s]] passing away and to have carried away [[Vrani|Vṛṣṇi]] women (Mausala 8. 16-17). Vide also the same account in [[Vinnupurāṇa V,|Viṣṇupurāṇa V.]] 38. 12-28, Matsya [[273, 18|273. 18]] speaks of ten [[Abhira|Ābhīra]] kings. The Khasa tribe had the custom of a brother [[war- . rying|marrying]] his deceased brother’s widow. Vide H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] vol. III. p. 861 [[A.|n.]] 1671. ↩︎

  140. Vide G. [[H,|H.]] Westcott on ‘Kabir and [[Kabir-panth|Kabir-panth’]] [[(Cawapore,|(Cawnpore,]] 1907) and ‘Kabir and his followers’ by F. E. Keay (1931). Kabir’s [[teach ing|teaching]] was an amalgam of both Hindu and Moslem ideas. He [[preached, ā|preached a]] doctrine of theism that did not tolerate polytheism, incarnations and idols. Kabir is said to have been a disciple of [[Rāmāpanda|Rāmānanda]] (who lived about 1400-1470 A. D.), an ascetic who preached at Benares that God should be worshiped under the name of Rāma. Kabir [[beld|held]] to the doctrines of Karma and Transmigration. He believed in the unity of God, but makes use of many names such as Rāma, Khudā, Allah, [[Sakti lo|Śakti in]] speaking of [[Him. :!:|Him.]] ↩︎ ↩︎

  151. The proposition that God is only one, that He is worshipped under various perceptible forms for concentrating the mind on Him, worship or contemplation of a formless object being difficult, are doctrines taught in the [[Purāgas|Purāṇas]] in spite of the fact that [[tbey|they]] preach worship of [[Viṣaa,|Viṣṇu,]] [[Kroa,|Kṛṣṇa,]] Śiva, [[Devi|Devī]] and others. Vide [[H, of Dh,|H. of Dh.]] vol. II pp. 714-715 and pp. 118-119 above. The [[facusema|विष्णुधर्मोत्तर]] I. 65. 32 states: [[Firehta|साकारे बद्धलक्षस्तु शून्यं]] [[शक्रोति|शक्नोति]] चिन्तितुम् । अन्यथा तु [[सुकट स्यानिरालम्बस्य|सुकष्टं स्यान्निरालम्बस्य]] चिन्तनम् [[।|॥;]] [[ride|vide]] also [[विष्णुधर्मोचर|विष्णुधर्मोत्तर]] III. [[108, 23|108. 23]] ff, particularly the verse [[Aral T AERUM FIT uta ta: Haut wala man TIE #|आदौ कृत्वा महाभागे साकारे लक्षबन्धनम् । ततः समर्थो भवति शून्ये ध्याने नरोत्तम ॥]] 26. Indian theologians and philosophers deliberately spared lower forms of worship with the thought that it was better to begin at a lower rung of the ladder to the highest goal than not to have a footing at all. A well-known verse is : [[gut ferat a ant at Hoitforum TAATETTU Stat vifarani na R: # RESTOT 62. 5 q. by spices|अग्नौ क्रियावतां देवो दिवि देवो मनीषिणाम् । प्रतिमास्वल्पबुद्धीनां योगिनां हृदये हरिः ॥ नृसिंहपुराण 62. 5 q. by अपरार्क]] on p. 140. ↩︎

  152. [[वंच|त्वं च]] रुद्र महाबाहो [[मोहशाखाणि|मोहशास्त्राणि]] कारय । अल्पायासं [[वर्शयित्वा मोहयात्रु महेश्वरः (२)|दर्शयित्वा मोहयाशु महेश्वरः (र ?) ॥]] वराह 70. 36; this verse and several more are quoted from वराह by अपरार्क p. 1052; ये वेदमार्गनिर्मुक्तास्तेषां मोहार्थमेव च । [[नयसिद्धान्तसंज्ञाभिर्मया शाकंतु वशिवम् ।|नयसिद्धान्तसञ्ज्ञाभिर्मया शास्त्रं तु दर्शितम् ॥]] वराह 70. 41; [[वं हि कद्र|त्वं हि रुद्र]] महाबाहो मोहनार्थे सुरद्विषाम् । पाखण्डाचरणं [[धर्म कुरुष्य महामते|धर्मं कुरुष्व च महामते ॥]] तामसानि पुराणानि कथयात्र च तान् [[प्रति।|प्रति ।]] मोहनानि च [[शास्राणि कुरुष्व च महामते पस|शास्त्राणि कुरुष्व च महामते ॥ पद्म]] VI. [[263. 24-25:|263. 24–25;]] तथा पाशुपतं शास्त्रं त्वमेव कुरु [[मुवत। कङ्कालशेवपापण्ड महाशैवादिभेदतः|सुव्रत । कङ्कालशैवपाषण्डमहाशैवादिभेदतः]] । अवलक्ष्य मतं [[सम्यम्वेदबा|सम्यग्वेदबाह्यं]] द्विजाधमाः । भस्मास्थिधारिणः सर्वे भविष्यन्ति न [[संशयः पम VI. 263.32-33. कङ्कालशs|संशयः ॥ पद्म VI. 263. 32–33. कङ्कालशैवs]] are the कापालिकs. Compare similar passages in [[Kurma|Kūrma]] I. 12. 261-62; [[Devibhagavata|Devībhāgavata]] VII. 39. 26-31 ([[अन्यानि पानि सामाणि लोकस्मिन् विविधानिच श्रुतिस्मृतिविरुद्धानि तामसान्येव सर्वशः। वामकापालक चैव कौलफ मैरवागमः ॥ शिवेन मोहनार्थाय प्रणीतो नान्यहेतुकः। “शेवाश्च पैम्णवाब सौराः शाकास्तथैव च । गाणपत्या आगमाश्च प्रणीता शङ्करेण तु तत्र वेदाविरुद्धोंशोप्युक्त एवं कचिद कचित् । वैदिकैस्तद्ग्रहे दोषो न भवत्येव कार्दचित्).|अन्यानि यानि शास्त्राणि लोकेऽस्मिन् विविधानि च ॥ श्रुतिस्मृतिविरुद्धानि तामसान्येव सर्वशः । वामकापालकं चैव कौलकं भैरवागमः ॥ शिवेन मोहनार्थाय प्रणीतो नान्यहेतुकः । … शैवाश्च वैष्णवाश्चैव सौराः शाक्तास्तथैव च । गाणपत्या आगमाश्च प्रणीता शङ्करेण तु ॥ तत्र वेदाविरुद्धोंशोप्युक्त एव क्वचित् क्वचित् । वैदिकैस्तद्ग्रहे दोषो न भवत्येव कर्हिचित् ॥).]] ↩︎

  158. शृणु देवि प्रवक्ष्यामि [[नाम्नामष्टोत्तर शतम्|नाम्नामष्टोत्तरं शतम्]] । सहस्रनाम्नां पुण्यानां त्रिरावृत्या तु यत्फलम् । एकावृत्या तु कृष्णस्य [[नामक तत्मयच्छति|नामैकं तत्प्रयच्छति]] । तस्मात्पुण्यतरं चैतत् स्तोत्रं [[पातक नाशनम्|पातकनाशनम्]] ॥ ब्रह्माण्ड III. 36. 18-20. Verses 21-41 set out the 108 names of [[Krana.|Kṛṣṇa.]] The one thousand and eight names of [[Vinnu|Viṣṇu]] are set out in [[Maba bharata,|Mahābhārata,]] [[Anusasanaparva|Anuśāsanaparva]] 149. 14-120 and the Garuḍa-purāṇa (I. 15. 1-160) contains names of [[विष्ण,|विष्णु,]] but often different from those in अनुशासन. ↩︎

  170. The Master [[( Śri|(Śrī]] [[Aurobindo )|Aurobindo)]] and the pupil are at variance about the poetic character of the [[Mantras,|Mantras.]] [[Śri|Śrī]] Aurobindo (p. XXXIV) after saying that his translation is a literary and not a strictly literal one speaks of the hymns as ‘great poetry’, magnificent in their [[colourlag|colouring]] and images, [[poble|noble]] and beautiful in rhythm. Now let us hear the disciple. On p. [[63|65]] Mr. Kapali Sastry states [[the|‘the]] hymnal poetry is unusual, different from other poetry, even from the most superb specimen’ and then he chides the readers and men like the present author and impliedly his own Master [[it|‘it]] is not permissible (why?) to class Vedic hymns with poetry of a literary and aesthetic [[kłod’.|kind’.]] On p. 31 Mr. Sastry asserts that [[mantras|‘mantras]] have two meanings, the inner which is psychological or spiritual and the true meaning and a gross or external meaning for common men’ and he adds that the use of words with double meanings was deliberate but was [[sffortless|effortless]] and natural. ↩︎

  177. The present author is aware that thousands of people look upon [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo as a yogin, a prophet and as a World Teacher. But the search for truth is higher than all [[propbets|prophets]] and [[yogios|yogins]] put [[togetber.|together.]] [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo and his disciple claim that he has received a new revelation about the Veda beyond the ken of all scholars of antiquity and modern times and they have very severely [[haadled|handled]] [[Yaska,|Yāska,]] Jaimini, Sāyaṇa and other commentators. The devotees and admirers of [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo must allow the same freedom to the critics of [[Sri|Śrī]] Aurobindo’s Vedic [[lacubrations|lucubrations]] for showing how and why they are wrong and are requested not to take umbrage at what [[tbo|the]] present writer [[bas|has]] said about his theories but only to reply to the [[aathor’s|author’s]] brief [[criticismo|criticisms]] on their merits or [[demerits,|demerits.]] ↩︎

  184. The passage of Matsya [[( 285.|(285.]] [[6–7)|6-7)]] is very likely a [[later loter polation,|later interpolation,]] because in another place in the Matsya, the names of avatāras are different. In [[Mataya|Matsya]] 47. 106 there is a reference to the curse on [[Viggo|Viṣṇu]] by [[Bhrga|Bhṛgu]] that since [[Viṣgu kūled|Viṣṇu killed]] his wife he would have to be [[bora|born]] seven times as a human being and the seven avatāras are [[Dattatreya,|Dattātreya,]] [[Māndbāta,|Māndhātā,]] [[Jāmadagoya (Bhargava)|Jāmadagnya (Bhārgava)]] [[Rama,|Rāma,]] Rāma Dāśarathi, Vedavyāsa, [[Baddba,|Buddha,]] [[Kalkio|Kalkin]] and three more [[(io|in]] [[47,|47.]] [[237–240 ),|237-240),]] viz. [[Nārāyaga,|Nārāyaṇa,]] [[Narasimha|Narasiṁha]] and [[Vimana|Vāmana]] are [[added: 1o|added; in]] Matsya 54. 15-19, [[Nakgatrapuruṣa-vrata|Nakṣatrapuruṣa-vrata]] is described and the usual ten avatāras are [[named,|named.]] ↩︎
